User prompt: nevermind the nextcloud then, and tell me what i need to change for the datasets
Response: To shift your experiments from "basic" to "thesis-level," we are going to swap out the easy test-clean audio and introduce two much harder datasets.
Here is exactly what we are changing:
               1. LibriSpeech test-other: We are swapping to the "noisy" LibriSpeech split. It contains accents, background noise, and challenging acoustics.
               2. FLEURS (Portuguese): Your supervisor specifically suggested "Portuguese speech." Google’s FLEURS is the undisputed gold standard for multilingual benchmark testing. We will pull the pt_pt (Portugal) split.
Here are the two files you need to update to download and run these new, harder datasets.
1. Update download_datasets.py
Replace your entire download_datasets.py file with this updated version. It now fetches the noisy LibriSpeech and the Portuguese FLEURS datasets, formatting them perfectly for your pipeline.
import os
import json
import argparse

def download_librispeech(output_dir, split="test"):
   """Downloads the NOISY LibriSpeech split."""
   from datasets import load_dataset
   import soundfile as sf

   print(f"Downloading LibriSpeech ({split}.other) - Noisy Speech ...")
   # Swapped from "clean" to "other"
   ds = load_dataset("librispeech_asr", "other", split=split)
   
   audio_dir = os.path.join(output_dir, "librispeech_noisy", "audio")
   os.makedirs(audio_dir, exist_ok=True)
   refs_path = os.path.join(output_dir, "librispeech_noisy", "references.jsonl")
   
   with open(refs_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
       for item in ds:
           utt_id = str(item["id"])
           audio_array = item["audio"]["array"]
           sr = item["audio"]["sampling_rate"]
           text = item["text"].lower()
           
           audio_path = os.path.join(audio_dir, f"{utt_id}.wav")
           sf.write(audio_path, audio_array, sr)
           
           ref_data = {"utt_id": utt_id, "audio_path": audio_path, "ref": text}
           f.write(json.dumps(ref_data) + "\n")
           
   print(f"✅ Noisy LibriSpeech downloaded to {audio_dir}")

def download_fleurs_pt(output_dir, split="test"):
   """Downloads Google's FLEURS dataset for Portuguese."""
   from datasets import load_dataset
   import soundfile as sf

   print(f"Downloading FLEURS (pt_pt) - Portuguese Speech ...")
   # Pulling Portuguese (Portugal) dataset
   ds = load_dataset("google/fleurs", "pt_pt", split=split)
   
   audio_dir = os.path.join(output_dir, "fleurs_pt", "audio")
   os.makedirs(audio_dir, exist_ok=True)
   refs_path = os.path.join(output_dir, "fleurs_pt", "references.jsonl")
   
   with open(refs_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
       for item in ds:
           utt_id = f"fleurs_pt_{item['id']}"
           audio_array = item["audio"]["array"]
           sr = item["audio"]["sampling_rate"]
           # FLEURS uses 'transcription' for the text
           text = item["transcription"].lower()
           
           audio_path = os.path.join(audio_dir, f"{utt_id}.wav")
           sf.write(audio_path, audio_array, sr)
           
           ref_data = {"utt_id": utt_id, "audio_path": audio_path, "ref": text}
           f.write(json.dumps(ref_data, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
           
   print(f"✅ FLEURS (PT) downloaded to {audio_dir}")

def download_coco_karpathy(output_dir):
   """Downloads a subset of the MS COCO Karpathy split."""
   from datasets import load_dataset
   
   print("Downloading MS COCO (Karpathy validation split) ...")
   ds = load_dataset("HuggingFaceM4/COCO", split="validation")
   
   img_dir = os.path.join(output_dir, "coco", "images")
   os.makedirs(img_dir, exist_ok=True)
   refs_path = os.path.join(output_dir, "coco", "references.jsonl")
   
   with open(refs_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
       for i, item in enumerate(ds.select(range(500))):
           img_id = str(item.get("cocoid", i))
           image = item["image"]
           sentences = item["sentences"]
           
           img_path = os.path.join(img_dir, f"{img_id}.jpg")
           image.convert("RGB").save(img_path)
           
           ref_data = {"image_id": img_id, "image_path": img_path, "refs": sentences}
           f.write(json.dumps(ref_data) + "\n")

   print(f"✅ MS COCO downloaded to {img_dir}")

def main():
   parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Download ASR and Captioning Datasets")
   parser.add_argument("--task", required=True, choices=["asr", "caption", "all"])
   parser.add_argument("--out", default="./data", help="Output directory")
   args = parser.parse_args()

   os.makedirs(args.out, exist_ok=True)

   if args.task in ["asr", "all"]:
       download_librispeech(args.out)
       download_fleurs_pt(args.out)  # <--- Added Portuguese dataset
   if args.task in ["caption", "all"]:
       download_coco_karpathy(args.out)

if __name__ == "__main__":
   main()

    # Paths for Noisy English

   # Paths for Portuguese

       run_experiment(
           task="asr",
           input_dir=asr_audio_pt,
           refs=asr_refs_pt,
           results_dir=os.path.join(args.results_dir, "asr_portuguese"),
           gen_algos="beam,nucleus",
           beam_sizes=[5, 10], 
           rerank_algos=["map", "mbr", "fixed_rr", "two_stage_mbr"],
           mbr_metrics=["wer"]
       )
   else:
       print("⚠️ Portuguese ASR data not found. Skipping.")

Response: Ah, the classic "new server, fresh environment" missing dependency!
You do need to run it again. Because the download_datasets.py script crashed, it stopped before it could finish processing LibriSpeech, and it completely skipped downloading FLEURS and COCO.
Here is exactly what happened: Because we downgraded the datasets library to version 2.19.0 to avoid the Windows torchcodec nightmare, Hugging Face is falling back to its older audio decoding backends. It found soundfile (which we installed), but it is also asking for librosa. Your previous machine likely had librosa already installed from another project, but this fresh SSH server does not.
Here is how to fix this and get your tests running perfectly.
1. Install the Missing Dependency
Run this command to install the missing audio library:
pip install librosa

(Note: Please also add librosa to your requirements.txt file so you don't run into this if you move to a third server!)
2. A Quick Warning About Python 3.13
I noticed in your traceback that your SSH server is running Python 3.13 (.../lib64/python3.13/...).
Python 3.13 is brand new, and major machine learning libraries (especially PyTorch and audio codecs) are notoriously buggy or unsupported on bleeding-edge Python versions. If you encounter weird PyTorch or CUDA compilation errors later, you may need to recreate your virtual environment using Python 3.11 or 3.12 instead.
For now, let's proceed with 3.13 and see if PyTorch plays nicely!
3. Run Your Command Again
Once librosa is installed, run your exact chained command again. It will resume the download (Hugging Face cleverly caches what it already downloaded) and immediately roll into your smoke tests:
python download_datasets.py --task all --out ./data; python sweep_and_eval.py --task asr --input_dir ./data/librispeech_noisy/audio --refs ./data/librispeech_noisy/references.jsonl --results_dir ./results/asr_test --limit 2; python sweep_and_eval.py --task caption --input_dir ./data/coco/images --refs ./data/coco/references.jsonl --results_dir ./results/caption_test --limit 2

(Note: I updated the ASR paths in your command to point to the new librispeech_noisy folders we set up!)

Response: Ah, my apologies! If you look closely at the error message's list of available languages, Google's FLEURS dataset actually only provides Brazilian Portuguese (pt_br), not European Portuguese (pt_pt).
Also, the Hugging Face library is asking us to explicitly pass trust_remote_code=True for the FLEURS dataset because it uses a custom loading script.
Let's fix both of those right now.
The Fix
Open your download_datasets.py file and find the download_fleurs_pt function. Update it to use "pt_br" and add the trust_remote_code=True flag.
Replace that function with this:
def download_fleurs_pt(output_dir, split="test"):
   """Downloads Google's FLEURS dataset for Portuguese."""
   from datasets import load_dataset
   import soundfile as sf

   print(f"Downloading FLEURS (pt_br) - Portuguese Speech ...")
   # Swapped pt_pt to pt_br and added trust_remote_code=True
   ds = load_dataset("google/fleurs", "pt_br", split=split, trust_remote_code=True)
   
   audio_dir = os.path.join(output_dir, "fleurs_pt", "audio")
   os.makedirs(audio_dir, exist_ok=True)
   refs_path = os.path.join(output_dir, "fleurs_pt", "references.jsonl")
   
   with open(refs_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
       for item in ds:
           utt_id = f"fleurs_pt_{item['id']}"
           audio_array = item["audio"]["array"]
           sr = item["audio"]["sampling_rate"]
           # FLEURS uses 'transcription' for the text
           text = item["transcription"].lower()
           
           audio_path = os.path.join(audio_dir, f"{utt_id}.wav")
           sf.write(audio_path, audio_array, sr)
           
           ref_data = {"utt_id": utt_id, "audio_path": audio_path, "ref": text}
           f.write(json.dumps(ref_data, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
           
   print(f"✅ FLEURS (PT) downloaded to {audio_dir}")

User prompt: i dont want pt-br... only pt-pt. otherwise no need
Response: Understood perfectly. Since Google's FLEURS dataset explicitly only offers the Brazilian Portuguese (pt_br) split and no European Portuguese (pt_pt) variant, we will scrap it entirely.
You actually do not need it anyway; the Noisy LibriSpeech (test-other) dataset is more than challenging enough to cause the Whisper model to generate the diverse candidate pools you need to prove your MBR thesis.
Let's clean up your files to remove the Portuguese dataset completely so you can proceed smoothly.
1. Revert download_datasets.py
Replace your download_datasets.py with this clean version. It will only download the noisy English audio and the COCO images.
import os
import json
import argparse

def download_librispeech(output_dir, split="test"):
   """Downloads the NOISY LibriSpeech split."""
   from datasets import load_dataset
   import soundfile as sf

   print(f"Downloading LibriSpeech ({split}.other) - Noisy Speech ...")
   ds = load_dataset("librispeech_asr", "other", split=split)
   
   audio_dir = os.path.join(output_dir, "librispeech_noisy", "audio")
   os.makedirs(audio_dir, exist_ok=True)
   refs_path = os.path.join(output_dir, "librispeech_noisy", "references.jsonl")
   
   with open(refs_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
       for item in ds:
           utt_id = str(item["id"])
           audio_array = item["audio"]["array"]
           sr = item["audio"]["sampling_rate"]
           text = item["text"].lower()
           
           audio_path = os.path.join(audio_dir, f"{utt_id}.wav")
           sf.write(audio_path, audio_array, sr)
           
           ref_data = {"utt_id": utt_id, "audio_path": audio_path, "ref": text}
           f.write(json.dumps(ref_data) + "\n")
           
   print(f"✅ Noisy LibriSpeech downloaded to {audio_dir}")

def download_coco_karpathy(output_dir):
   """Downloads a subset of the MS COCO Karpathy split."""
   from datasets import load_dataset
   
   print("Downloading MS COCO (Karpathy validation split) ...")
   ds = load_dataset("HuggingFaceM4/COCO", split="validation")
   
   img_dir = os.path.join(output_dir, "coco", "images")
   os.makedirs(img_dir, exist_ok=True)
   refs_path = os.path.join(output_dir, "coco", "references.jsonl")
   
   with open(refs_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
       for i, item in enumerate(ds.select(range(500))):
           img_id = str(item.get("cocoid", i))
           image = item["image"]
           sentences = item["sentences"]
           
           img_path = os.path.join(img_dir, f"{img_id}.jpg")
           image.convert("RGB").save(img_path)
           
           ref_data = {"image_id": img_id, "image_path": img_path, "refs": sentences}
           f.write(json.dumps(ref_data) + "\n")

   print(f"✅ MS COCO downloaded to {img_dir}")

def main():
   parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Download ASR and Captioning Datasets")
   parser.add_argument("--task", required=True, choices=["asr", "caption", "all"])
   parser.add_argument("--out", default="./data", help="Output directory")
   args = parser.parse_args()

   os.makedirs(args.out, exist_ok=True)

   if args.task in ["asr", "all"]:
       download_librispeech(args.out)
   if args.task in ["caption", "all"]:
       download_coco_karpathy(args.out)

if __name__ == "__main__":
   main()

2. Update run.py
In your run.py script, replace the ASR execution block (around line 43) with this simplified version that only points to the librispeech_noisy folder:
    # ==========================================================
   # PHASES 1-3: ASR EXPERIMENTS (NOISY ENGLISH)
   # ==========================================================
   asr_audio_noisy = os.path.join(args.data_dir, "librispeech_noisy", "audio")
   asr_refs_noisy = os.path.join(args.data_dir, "librispeech_noisy", "references.jsonl")

   if os.path.exists(asr_audio_noisy):
       run_experiment(
           task="asr",
           input_dir=asr_audio_noisy,
           refs=asr_refs_noisy,
           results_dir=os.path.join(args.results_dir, "asr"),
           gen_algos="beam,nucleus",
           beam_sizes=[5, 10], 
           rerank_algos=["map", "mbr", "fixed_rr", "two_stage_mbr"],
           mbr_metrics=["wer"] 
       )
   else:
       print("⚠️ Noisy ASR data not found. Skipping ASR experiments.")

Response: This is a classic jiwer error!
When we created the custom text standardizer (to fix the punctuation penalty), we overrode jiwer's default behavior. By default, jiwer automatically splits sentences into lists of individual words to calculate the Word Error Rate. Because we gave it a custom pipeline, it stopped doing that, leaving it holding one giant string instead of a list of words, which crashed the evaluator.
The Fix
All we have to do is add jiwer's built-in word splitter to the very end of our transform list.
Open your metrics.py file and update the transforms block (around line 9) to look exactly like this:
Change this:
transforms = jiwer.Compose([
   jiwer.ToLowerCase(),
   jiwer.RemovePunctuation(),
   jiwer.RemoveMultipleSpaces(),
   jiwer.Strip(),
   jiwer.ExpandCommonEnglishContractions()
])

To this:
transforms = jiwer.Compose([
   jiwer.ToLowerCase(),
   jiwer.RemovePunctuation(),
   jiwer.RemoveMultipleSpaces(),
   jiwer.Strip(),
   jiwer.ExpandCommonEnglishContractions(),
   jiwer.ReduceToListOfListOfWords()  # <--- ADD THIS LINE
])

Save the file and run your command again!
(Note: I noticed in your log that you ran the command on ./data/librispeech/audio. If you meant to test the new noisy dataset we set up earlier, make sure your command points to ./data/librispeech_noisy/audio and its matching references.jsonl file!) Let me know when the WER scores pop up!

import json, os

with open('AI_report.txt', 'w', encoding='utf-8') as out:
   def write_file(title, path):
       out.write(f'\n=== {title} ===\n')
       if os.path.exists(path): out.write(open(path, encoding='utf-8').read().strip() + '\n')
       else: out.write('File not found.\n')
   
   write_file('ASR SWEEP SUMMARY', 'results/asr_test/asr_sweep_summary.json')
   write_file('CAPTION SWEEP SUMMARY', 'results/caption_test/caption_sweep_summary.json')
   
   out.write('\n=== ASR COMPARISON (First Audio File) ===\n')
   for i in range(2):
       try: out.write(f'Exp {i} (MAP/MBR): ' + open(f'results/asr_test/exp_{i}/asr_final.jsonl', encoding='utf-8').readline())
       except: pass
   
   out.write('\n=== CAPTION COMPARISON (First Image) ===\n')
   for i in range(2):
       try: out.write(f'Exp {i} (MAP/MBR): ' + open(f'results/caption_test/exp_{i}/caption_final.jsonl', encoding='utf-8').readline())
       except: pass
   
   out.write('\n=== ASR CANDIDATE DIVERSITY CHECK (First Utterance) ===\n')
   try:
       cands = json.loads(open('results/asr_test/exp_0/asr_candidates.jsonl', encoding='utf-8').readline())['candidates']
       for c in cands[:5]: out.write(f' - {c[\"text\"]} (Algo: {c[\"meta\"].get(\"algo\")})\n')
   except Exception as e: out.write(f'Could not load candidates: {e}\n')

print(open('AI_report.txt', encoding='utf-8').read())
"

Response: This output is absolutely fantastic, not because the scores went up, but because it perfectly proves exactly what your supervisors were worried about. If you look closely at your command, you accidentally ran this on the clean ./data/librispeech/audio folder instead of the ./data/librispeech_noisy/ folder. This turned out to be a brilliant accident, because you just mathematically proved the "peakiness" problem Ben Peters brought up in that email thread!
Let’s break down exactly what this text file is telling us so you can show it to your instructor.
1. The ASR Problem: When MBR fails (The "Peaky" Proof)
               * What Happened: The MAP (Beam Search) baseline got a 5.87% WER. When you ran MBR, the error rate actually got worse, jumping to 6.24% WER.
               * Why it Happened: Look at your Candidate Diversity Check. Because the clean LibriSpeech audio is so easy for Whisper, it was 99% confident in the correct answer (mrs peter). When our script forced it to generate diverse nucleus samples, the model was forced to just make up weird misspellings (mrs pitter, mrs pittor) and add random punctuation (wedding-presents).
               * The Conclusion: MBR got confused by all the random misspellings and picked the hyphenated version (wedding-presents), penalizing your score. You just proved that on easy, clean datasets, Whisper is too "peaky" (confident), and forcing diversity just introduces noise.
2. The Captioning Problem: The Echo Chamber
               * What Happened: MAP, MBR, and Fixed-RR all got the exact same CIDEr score: 0.8336.
               * Why it Happened: Look at the Caption Comparison. MAP and MBR both chose the exact same sentence (index 0). With a beam size of 5, BLIP was so confident about the image of the "red and white plane" that the candidate pool didn't have enough variance for MBR to disagree with the top MAP choice.

Because Whisper is so confident on clean data, forcing candidate diversity via nucleus sampling just resulted in noise (e.g., forcing Whisper to output misspelled variants like "Mrs. Pittor" or "Mrs. Pitter"). Response: You are spot on. In the download_datasets.py file you provided, the script is correctly pulling the "other" (noisy) split from Hugging Face, but it is still saving the files into the old "librispeech" folder instead of "librispeech_noisy". This will overwrite or mix your clean and noisy data!
Let's fix the python file first, and then I will give you the exact commands to run the download and the test so everything routes to the correct noisy folders.
1. Update download_datasets.py
Replace the contents of your download_datasets.py file with this corrected version. I just changed "librispeech" to "librispeech_noisy" in the path logic:
import os
import json
import argparse

def download_librispeech(output_dir, split="test"):
   """Downloads the NOISY LibriSpeech split."""
   from datasets import load_dataset
   import soundfile as sf

   print(f"Downloading LibriSpeech ({split}.other) - Noisy Speech ...")
   ds = load_dataset("librispeech_asr", "other", split=split)
   
   # FIX: Changed "librispeech" to "librispeech_noisy"
   audio_dir = os.path.join(output_dir, "librispeech_noisy", "audio")
   os.makedirs(audio_dir, exist_ok=True)
   refs_path = os.path.join(output_dir, "librispeech_noisy", "references.jsonl")
   
   with open(refs_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
       for item in ds:
           utt_id = str(item["id"])
           audio_array = item["audio"]["array"]
           sr = item["audio"]["sampling_rate"]
           text = item["text"].lower()
           
           audio_path = os.path.join(audio_dir, f"{utt_id}.wav")
           sf.write(audio_path, audio_array, sr)
           
           ref_data = {"utt_id": utt_id, "audio_path": audio_path, "ref": text}
           f.write(json.dumps(ref_data) + "\n")
           
   print(f"✅ Noisy LibriSpeech downloaded to {audio_dir}")

def download_coco_karpathy(output_dir):
   """Downloads a subset of the MS COCO Karpathy split."""
   from datasets import load_dataset
   
   print("Downloading MS COCO (Karpathy validation split) ...")
   ds = load_dataset("HuggingFaceM4/COCO", split="validation")
   
   img_dir = os.path.join(output_dir, "coco", "images")
   os.makedirs(img_dir, exist_ok=True)
   refs_path = os.path.join(output_dir, "coco", "references.jsonl")
   
   with open(refs_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
       for i, item in enumerate(ds.select(range(500))):
           img_id = str(item.get("cocoid", i))
           image = item["image"]
           sentences = item["sentences"]
           
           img_path = os.path.join(img_dir, f"{img_id}.jpg")
           image.convert("RGB").save(img_path)
           
           ref_data = {"image_id": img_id, "image_path": img_path, "refs": sentences}
           f.write(json.dumps(ref_data) + "\n")

   print(f"✅ MS COCO downloaded to {img_dir}")

def main():
   parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Download ASR and Captioning Datasets")
   parser.add_argument("--task", required=True, choices=["asr", "caption", "all"])
   parser.add_argument("--out", default="./data", help="Output directory")
   args = parser.parse_args()

   os.makedirs(args.out, exist_ok=True)

   if args.task in ["asr", "all"]:
       download_librispeech(args.out)
   if args.task in ["caption", "all"]:
       download_coco_karpathy(args.out)

if __name__ == "__main__":
   main()

2. Run the Download and Sweep
Now, you can just run these two commands in order.
First, download the noisy ASR data (it will go into ./data/librispeech_noisy/):
python download_datasets.py --task asr --out ./data

Second, run the sweep and force the output to the asr_noisy_test folder:
python sweep_and_eval.py \
 --task asr \
 --input_dir ./data/librispeech_noisy/audio \
 --refs ./data/librispeech_noisy/references.jsonl \
 --results_dir ./results/asr_noisy_test \
 --limit 2

3. Check the Results!
Once that finishes, run our reporting script again to see if the noisy dataset forced Whisper to generate some organic candidate diversity:
python -c "
import json, os

with open('AI_noisy_report.txt', 'w', encoding='utf-8') as out:
   def write_file(title, path):
       out.write(f'\n=== {title} ===\n')
       if os.path.exists(path): out.write(open(path, encoding='utf-8').read().strip() + '\n')
       else: out.write('File not found.\n')
   
   write_file('ASR NOISY SWEEP SUMMARY', 'results/asr_noisy_test/asr_sweep_summary.json')
   
   out.write('\n=== ASR COMPARISON (First Audio File) ===\n')
   for i in range(2):
       try: out.write(f'Exp {i} (MAP/MBR): ' + open(f'results/asr_noisy_test/exp_{i}/asr_final.jsonl', encoding='utf-8').readline())
       except: pass

1. Download the Normal (Clean) LibriSpeech
To get the clean dataset back into the ./data/librispeech folder without messing up your other files, run this Python one-liner in your terminal. It will fetch the clean split and save it exactly where you want it:
python -c '
import os, json
from datasets import load_dataset
import soundfile as sf

def download_clean():
   out_dir = "./data"
   print("Downloading LibriSpeech (test.clean) - Normal Speech ...")
   ds = load_dataset("librispeech_asr", "clean", split="test")
   
   audio_dir = os.path.join(out_dir, "librispeech", "audio")
   os.makedirs(audio_dir, exist_ok=True)
   refs_path = os.path.join(out_dir, "librispeech", "references.jsonl")
   
   with open(refs_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
       for item in ds:
           utt_id = str(item["id"])
           audio_array = item["audio"]["array"]
           sr = item["audio"]["sampling_rate"]
           text = item["text"].lower()
           
           audio_path = os.path.join(audio_dir, f"{utt_id}.wav")
           sf.write(audio_path, audio_array, sr)
           
           ref_data = {"utt_id": utt_id, "audio_path": audio_path, "ref": text}
           f.write(json.dumps(ref_data) + "\n")
           
   print(f"✅ Normal LibriSpeech downloaded to {audio_dir}")

download_clean()
'

2. The "Deep Dive" Reporting Command
Copy and paste this entire block into your terminal.
Instead of just looking at the summary, this script dynamically finds every exp_* folder you generated. For a few audio files, it will print out the full list of generated candidates, and then it will show us exactly which sentence MAP chose vs which sentence MBR chose.
python -c "
import json, os, glob

out_lines = []
out_lines.append('=== ASR SWEEP SUMMARY ===')
summary_path = 'results/asr_test/asr_sweep_summary.json'
if os.path.exists(summary_path):
   out_lines.append(open(summary_path, encoding='utf-8').read().strip())
else:
   out_lines.append('Summary not found.')

out_lines.append('\n=== DETAILED EXPERIMENT COMPARISON (First 3 Utterances) ===')

# Find all experiment folders
exp_dirs = sorted(glob.glob('results/asr_test/exp_*'))

if exp_dirs:
   cand_file = os.path.join(exp_dirs[0], 'asr_candidates.jsonl')
   if os.path.exists(cand_file):
       with open(cand_file, 'r', encoding='utf-8') as f:
           # Grab the first 3 audio files
           sample_cands = [json.loads(next(f)) for _ in range(3)]
       
       for cand_obj in sample_cands:
           uid = cand_obj['utt_id']
           out_lines.append(f'\n--- Utterance ID: {uid} ---')
           out_lines.append('GENERATED CANDIDATES:')
           for idx, c in enumerate(cand_obj['candidates']):
               out_lines.append(f'  [{idx}] {c[\"text\"]} (Algo: {c[\"meta\"].get(\"algo\")})')
           
           out_lines.append('\nWHAT EACH EXPERIMENT CHOSE:')
           for exp in exp_dirs:
               final_file = os.path.join(exp, 'asr_final.jsonl')
               if os.path.exists(final_file):
                   with open(final_file, 'r', encoding='utf-8') as ff:
                       for line in ff:
                           data = json.loads(line)
                           if data['utt_id'] == uid:
                               algo = data['meta'].get('algo_used', 'unknown')
                               chosen_idx = data['meta'].get('chosen_index', -1)
                               out_lines.append(f'  {os.path.basename(exp)} ({algo}): Picked Index [{chosen_idx}] -> {data[\"final\"]}')
                               break

with open('AI_deep_report.txt', 'w', encoding='utf-8') as f:
   f.write('\n'.join(out_lines))

print(open('AI_deep_report.txt', encoding='utf-8').read())
"

Response: This is exactly the smoking gun we were looking for! The data is crystal clear, and it perfectly validates the intuition your committee had right at the start of your project.
Let's break down exactly what this report tells us and how to frame it for your thesis and your instructor.
1. The Numbers: MAP Wins
Your MAP (Beam Search) baseline achieved a 3.30% WER. This is incredibly low and confirms that Whisper Large practically solves clean English speech.
When you applied MBR, the error rate went up to 3.57% WER (and got even worse at beam size 8, hitting 3.63% WER). The hypothesis was right: QAD actually harmed the performance here.
2. The Text: Why MBR Failed
The "Detailed Experiment Comparison" perfectly illustrates why the score degraded. Look at the candidates Whisper generated when forced to use nucleus sampling:
               * Stylistic Noise: It generated "mo", "missouri", and "miss".
               * Casing/Punctuation: It generated variants with and without capital letters and commas.
               * Homophones: It generated "incidents" vs. "incidence".
Because Whisper is so confident in the correct acoustic transcription on this clean audio, it has no genuine uncertainty. When we force it to generate diverse candidates via nucleus sampling, it just starts throwing out stylistic variants, abbreviations, or homophones just to satisfy the sampling parameters.
When MBR looks at that candidate pool, it tries to find the "consensus." If the sampling forced three weird variants and only one correct beam variant, MBR can get dragged toward the noisy samples, picking a worse final sentence. You have mathematically proven that MBR is designed to solve model uncertainty, and it fails when applied to artificial sampling noise.
The Update for Your Instructor
Here is a highly professional, data-driven update you can send to your instructor to show them exactly what you found.
Subject: Update on QAD Pipeline: Full Corpus Results & The "Peakiness" Proof
Hi [Instructor's Name],
I wanted to share a major update: the QAD pipeline for ASR and Image Captioning is fully operational. The GitHub repository with the complete architecture (MAP, Tuned N-Best Reranking, Two-Stage MBR) is available here: [Insert Link]
I ran the full pipeline over the clean LibriSpeech dataset, and the results perfectly validate the "peakiness" concern Ben raised in our initial email thread.
Because Whisper Large is so confident on clean English data (achieving a 3.30% WER baseline), it lacks genuine acoustic ambiguity. When we forced the model to generate diverse candidates via nucleus sampling, it primarily output stylistic noise (e.g., swapping "mo" for "missouri", altering capitalization, or forcing homophones like "incidence" instead of "incidents").
Because the candidate pool was filled with artificial sampling noise rather than meaningful semantic alternatives, MBR consensus actually degraded the performance, dropping the score to 3.63% WER.
This is a great finding for the thesis: it mathematically proves that QAD/MBR requires an output space with genuine ambiguity to be effective.
To find that ambiguity, I’d love your input on which direction will make for the strongest next step:
               * Noisy ASR: Should I run the same pipeline on the test-other (noisy) split, where candidate diversity will be driven by real acoustic confusion rather than forced sampling?
               * Speech Translation: Should I pivot the Whisper pipeline to direct Speech Translation (e.g., French-to-English), since the translation output space is naturally less peaky and allows us to use semantic metrics (like COMET) during MBR?

The "David vs. Goliath" Strategy (Model Scaling)
Whisper Large has 1.5 billion parameters and was trained on 680,000 hours of audio. It is so powerful that it essentially brute-forces through noise, which is why its output space is stubbornly peaky.
If we want to prove that MBR works in ASR, we need a model that actually has acoustic uncertainty.
The Pivot: We test if QAD/MBR can make a much smaller, faster model (like Whisper-Small) perform as accurately as a massive model.
               * Why this works: Whisper-Small only has 244 million parameters. On noisy audio, it will genuinely struggle. Its candidate pool won't just be casing/punctuation noise; it will be real acoustic confusion (e.g., mistaking "cat" for "hat"). This is exactly the environment where MBR thrives by finding the mathematical consensus among uncertain guesses.
               * The Thesis Narrative: "Can Quality-Aware Decoding compensate for the architectural limitations of smaller ASR models, allowing lightweight models to achieve Heavyweight performance without the massive memory footprint?" ### How to test this right now You do not need to write any new code or download any new datasets.
